title: "United States support for Israel in the Gaza war"
---

# United States support for Israel in the Gaza war

|  | Parts of this article (those related to the Trump administration's support for Israel) need to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(May 2025)* |
| --- | --- |



The [White House](33057.md) illuminated in the colors of the [Israeli flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_flag) on 9 October 2023

The [United States](3434750.md) has provided extensive military, diplomatic, and financial support to [Israel](9282173.md) during the ongoing [Gaza war](74998519.md). This has included rapid deployment of warships and military aircraft, billions of dollars in military aid and expedited weapons shipments — including [guided missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guided_missiles), [artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artillery_shells), and advanced defense systems.

After an initial period of broader [Western](21208200.md) support for Israel's offensive, Israel and the United States have become increasingly isolated amid growing worldwide [calls for a ceasefire](75313349.md),[^cite_note-Time-2023-1][^cite_note-AP_News-2023-2][^cite_note-The_Guardian-2023-3] with the US [vetoing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_vetoed_United_Nations_Security_Council_resolutions) six [United Nations Security Council resolutions](178970.md) calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-4][^cite_note-5][^cite_note-6] International rights groups have condemned the U.S. for providing support to Israel which they say risks complicity in [Israeli war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes_in_the_Gaza_war) and violations of the [Leahy Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leahy_Law) and [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act_of_1974).[^cite_note-Lederer-2023-7][^cite_note-Ratcliffe-2023-8][^cite_note-9]

As the war continued, tensions between the Israeli government and the [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration) began to grow.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] Some in the US government became more publicly critical of Israel as Palestinian civilian [casualties](75130785.md) rose and opposition grew. Over a dozen U.S. officials resigned, and there were large-scale demonstrations on university campuses and outside defense contractors. In February 2024, the Biden administration issued a [national security directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_security_directive) requiring written assurances from Israel that it was using US-supplied weapons in line with international law. In March 2024, the US began calling for an immediate and sustained ceasefire linked to the release of hostages, and Israel criticized the US for allowing [a ceasefire resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2728) to pass at the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council). The US also voiced its opposition to much of Israel's post-war plan for Gaza.[^cite_note-12] Despite this, however, American weapons transfers to Israel continued.[^cite_note-13][^cite_note-14]

## Background

Further information: [7 October attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_October_attacks), [Gaza war](74998519.md), and [Israel–United States military relations § Military aid and procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations#Military_aid_and_procurement)



Graph showing recipients of [U.S. foreign aid](22557286.md), 1946–2022

[^cite_note-15]

After Hamas-led Palestinian armed groups attacked Israel on 7 October, resulting in the death of 1,195 Israelis, 828 of which were civilians, Israel began a military campaign against Gaza.[^cite_note-Holland-2023-16][^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] Since the start of the Israeli operation, more than 73,000 Palestinians in Gaza have been killed, of the whom at least half of those identified were [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Gaza_war) and [children](75987631.md), with at least 80% of the total dead being civilians.[^cite_note-19][^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21] Almost 7,400 Palestinians are missing and presumed trapped under rubble.[^cite_note-22] Israel placed Gaza under a [complete blockade](75013719.md), including the prevention of fuel and water from entering the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-Harb-2023-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] The United States described Hamas-led attack as "unprovoked,"[^cite_note-28] and started sending warships and warplanes into the region, prepared to give Israel whatever it needs.[^cite_note-Copp-2023-29] Civil rights groups such as the [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) meanwhile called on the government to address the underlying issues that have led to the recent violence, such as Israel's illegal [possession of the Palestinian lands](2781576.md) and its [blockade of Gaza](19224310.md) as well as "the [apartheid regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_apartheid) throughout historic Palestine".[^cite_note-Speri-2023-30]

Following Israel's victory over surrounding Arab forces in the [1967 Six-Day War](29328.md) and its subsequent occupation of the West Bank, East Jerusalem, and Gaza, military assistance to the country surged significantly. The [Iron Dome](18342427.md), which became operational in 2011, was created with the help of the United States, which is responsible for providing components for the system, including allocating more than \$1.5 billion for missile defense for Israel in 2022.[^cite_note-How_big_is_Israel-2023-31] As part of a [record \$38 billion agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_U.S.-Israel_Memorandum_of_Understanding) over ten years negotiated under former US President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) in 2016, US military aid to Israel exceeded \$3.8 billion in 2023. Of this \$3.8 billion, half a billion was for Israel's missile defense. Washington has announced that it will replenish Israel's ammunition used in the war.[^cite_note-How_big_is_Israel-2023-31]

## Military support

### Weapons transfers



[USAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAF) airmen prepare for departure in a [C-17 Globemaster III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-17_Globemaster_III) carrying ammunition to Israel, 15 October 2023, at [Ramstein Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramstein_Air_Base), Germany.

The Biden administration caused controversy after bypassing Congress on multiple occasions to authorize arms sales to the Israeli military.[^cite_note-32] Unlike the [United States' military support for Ukraine](70376708.md), details of weapons sent to Israel have been vague.[^cite_note-33] Leaked details have shown that the U.S. has sent [laser-guided missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser-guided_bomb), [155mm shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/155_mm_caliber), new army vehicles, among others, at Israel's request.[^cite_note-34] According to the [Costs of War Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costs_of_War_Project) at [Brown University](4157.md), the United States spent \$21.7 billion on military aid to Israel from October 2023 to September 2025.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36]

In December 2023, the WSJ report stated that US arms shipments to Israel since the start of the war included 15,000 bombs and 57,000 [155mm artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/155_mm_caliber). The United States also delivered 100 [BLU-109](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BLU-109_bomb) [bunker buster bombs](167751.md), 5,000 unguided [Mk82 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_82_bomb), more than 5,400 [Mk84 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_84_bomb), about 1,000 small diameter [GBU-39 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-39_Small_Diameter_Bomb), and approximately 3,000 [JDAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Direct_Attack_Munition) guidance kits.[^cite_note-US_sends-2023-37]

An investigation by *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* found the U.S. government was using special mechanisms to protect Israel from domestic human rights laws to continue sending weapons.[^cite_note-38] Following a trip to Washington D.C. in June 2024, Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) addressed Israeli complaints about slowed arms transfers, stating, "Obstacles were removed and bottlenecks were addressed".[^cite_note-39] A report by [ProPublica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProPublica) found the U.S. State Department had ignored reports about potential human rights violations by the Israeli army to continue weapons transfers to Israel.[^cite_note-40] Current and former State Department officials stated there were more than 500 reports of Israel using U.S. weapons to cause "unnecessary harm to civilians" in the Gaza Strip, but that no action had yet been taken on any of the reports.[^cite_note-41]

Unnamed officials stated in March 2024 that the U.S. had signed off on an additional 1,800 [MK84](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_84_bomb) 2,000-pound bombs and 500 [MK82](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_82_bomb) 500-pound bombs.[^cite_note-42] In May 2024, Israel used two U.S.-made [GBU-39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-39_Small_Diameter_Bomb) missiles during the [Tel al-Sultan massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_al-Sultan_massacre).[^cite_note-43] GBU-39 bombs were also identified in other attacks on dense civilian areas, including the [Al-Sardi school attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Sardi_school_attack) and the [Al-Tabaeen school attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Tabaeen_school_attack).[^cite_note-44] In June 2024, two U.S. officials stated the United States had transferred ten thousand 2,000-pound bombs and thousands of Hellfire missiles to Israel since 7 October.[^cite_note-45] In late-June 2024, an Israeli official stated the Biden administration would soon transfer a delayed shipment of 500 pound bombs to Israel.[^cite_note-46] In mid-July 2024, a U.S. official confirmed that the United States was resuming its transfers of 500 pound bombs to Israel.[^cite_note-47]

In August 2024, the Biden administration approved a \$20 billion arms sale to Israel, including F-15 fighter jets and tank and mortar shells.[^cite_note-48] The United States approved a \$165 million sale of military tank trailers, including replacement parts, tool kits, and logistics support.[^cite_note-49]

### Naval and air support

The United States has provided naval and aerial support to Israel on multiple occasions since October 2023. In April 2024, U.S. fighter jets, along with the U.K., France, and Jordan, shot down [Iranian drones and missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel) directed at Israel.[^cite_note-50] In June 2024, the United States sent the [USS *Wasp*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Wasp_\(LHD-1\)) and its marines aboard to serve as a deterrent to Hezbollah in its conflict with Israel.[^cite_note-51] In August 2024, the United States deployed the [USS *Abraham Lincoln* Carrier Strike Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Strike_Group_3), equipped with stealth [F-35C Lightning II](11812.md) combat jets, and the ballistic missile submarine [USS *Georgia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Georgia_\(SSGN-729\)), to Israel.[^cite_note-52]

In October 2024, the United States deployed the [Terminal High-Altitude Area Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_High-Altitude_Area_Defense), along with 100 U.S. troops to Israel.[^cite_note-53] That same month, an investigation using available open source data found that U.S. aircraft were responsible for 33 percent of [reconnaissance flights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconnaissance_aircraft), providing Israel with intelligence on ground movements in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-54] In November 2024, the United States ordered ballistic missile defense destroyers, a fighter squadron, tanker aircraft, and Air Force [B-52 long-range strike bombers](18933037.md) to "make clear" to Iran that the U.S. would "take every measure necessary to defend" its interests in the region.[^cite_note-55]

In response to the United States's involvement, U.S. Representatives [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib) and [Cori Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cori_Bush) wrote a letter to the Biden Administration asking for an explanation as to the country's participation in Israel's military engagements and suggesting such collaboration was unauthorized and unconstitutional.[^cite_note-56]

## Financial support

The United States has provided \$9 billion in loan guarantees to Israel.[^cite_note-57] Israel has also issued \$5 billion of debt in the U.S. during the war with state and local governments making up a large portion of the purchases.[^cite_note-58] Karen Wells *et al.* point to the billions in financial support as evidence of US complicity in Israel's "genocidal war".[^cite_note-59] Research in 2024 showed that Israel's military relies heavily on fuel imports from the US for its operations in Gaza. [Francesca Albanese](72566699.md), the [United Nations Special Rapporteur on the occupied Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_the_occupied_Palestinian_territories), said the United States' provision of fuel to Israel after the ICJ's provisional ruling was "a breach of the Genocide Convention".[^cite_note-60]

As of April 2024, state and local governments in the U.S. had purchased \$1.7 billion in [Israel Bonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Bonds) since 7 October 2023.[^cite_note-61] States which have purchased Israel bonds during the war include Florida, Illinois, Indiana, New York, and Texas. [Palm Beach County](73647.md) in Florida has purchased almost \$700 million in Israel bonds during the war, leading residents to file a lawsuit against the county comptroller [Joseph Abruzzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Abruzzo) for allegedly violating his fiduciary duty.[^cite_note-62] On 6 February 2025, President Donald Trump issued [Executive Order 14203](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Order_14203) imposing sanctions on the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) due to its [arrest warrants for Israeli leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders).[^cite_note-63] In June 2025, the United States imposed financial sanctions on four ICC judges including two who were part of the investigation of [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Yoav Gallant.[^cite_note-64] The US also imposed financial sanctions on Francesca Albanese after she issued a report on corporate involvement in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-65] On 20 August 2025, the US sanctioned ICC judge [Nicolas Guillou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Guillou) for authorizing the arrest warrants for Netanyahu and Gallant. The US also sanctioned ICC prosecutors [Nazhat Shameem Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazhat_Shameem) and Mame Mandiaye Niang for continuing to investigate Israel.[^cite_note-66]

## Protests



Demonstration outside [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation)'s office in [Goleta, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goleta,_California) to protest the military contractor's supply of weapons to Israel, 9 November 2023

Josh Paul, a senior State Department official specializing in arms transfers, resigned after stating the U.S. government continues to sell weapons to Israel despite its record of human rights abuses.[^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68]

"[Direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action)" tactics were adopted against [arms companies](608296.md) in the United States that supplied arms to Israel, including [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin),[^cite_note-69] [General Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics),[^cite_note-70] [Textron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textron),[^cite_note-71] [Boeing](18933266.md),[^cite_note-72] [L3Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L3Harris),[^cite_note-73] [Raytheon Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation) and [Northrop Grumman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman).[^cite_note-74]

[War protesters on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_the_United_States_in_2024) are calling for universities to withdraw any funding from arms manufacturers and companies linked to the Israeli military,[^cite_note-75] including [Cisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisco), [Caterpillar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.) and [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric).[^cite_note-76]

A group of seven [Democrat](5043544.md) U.S. senators stated that the Biden administration was in violation of the [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act), which stipulates that weapons cannot be transferred to governments blocking humanitarian assistance.[^cite_note-77] Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) called on Biden to cease weapons transfers, stating, "We need the president and the Biden administration to push harder and to use all the levers of US policy to ensure people don't die of starvation".[^cite_note-78]

In June 2024, the [NAACP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NAACP) called on the Biden administration to stop sending weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-79]



Pro-Palestinian protest against the American chain [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks) in [Exeter, England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exeter,_England), 10 February 2024

In July 2024, a group of U.S. doctors and nurses returning from Gaza stated they had witnessed widespread war crimes committed by the Israeli army and called on the United States to implement an arms embargo.[^cite_note-80] In October 2024, a majority of all American doctors who had volunteered in Gaza in the prior year signed a letter to the Biden administration calling for an arms embargo.[^cite_note-81] Later the same month, the editorial board of the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* called for an U.S. arms embargo on Israel, stating, "Biden has the tools to rein in Netanyahu. He must halt the offensive arms sales to Israel that enable its relentless bombing of Gaza and Lebanon."[^cite_note-82]

In May 2025, a pro-Palestinian protest was held in [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver), Colorado against Congressman [Jason Crow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Crow) for repeatedly accepting campaign donations from [Palantir Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palantir_Technologies), which provides intelligence and surveillance services to the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-83]

## Human rights issues

[Joe Biden](145422.md) delivering remarks on [Hamas](13913.md)'s [attack on Israel](74998317.md), October 2023

The [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) on 27 October 2023 voted a non-binding resolution calling for a humanitarian ceasefire to enable relief to enter Gaza; the United States stood with Israel in rejecting the proposal. The previous week, Washington had vetoed a resolution similar to this one at the more powerful UN Security Council.[^cite_note-84] Email correspondence between the Pentagon and White House in October 2023, however, showed U.S. officials were concerned about the risk of Israeli war crimes.[^cite_note-85]

Democratic congressman [Andre Carson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Carson) of Indiana wrote to *The Guardian*, accusing Israel of "war crimes" and citing the Israeli Defence Forces' (IDF) alleged use of white phosphorus and this week's deadly bombing of the [Jabalia refugee camp](18169472.md). "I am very concerned that our taxpayer dollars may be used for violations of human rights," Carson wrote.[^cite_note-86] The Biden administration declared earlier that week that it was not imposing any restrictions on Israel's use of the US-supplied weapons. According to Pentagon spokesman [Sabrina Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabrina_Singh), "That is really up to the Israel Defense Force to use in how they are going to conduct their operations... But we're not putting any constraints on that."[^cite_note-87]

Like Carson, Democratic representative [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez), particularly mentioned the alleged use of white phosphorus, as asserted by [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW), as a violation that should bar Israel from getting aid from the US. "Deployment of white phosphorus near populated civilian areas is a war crime," she said.[^cite_note-Tait-2023-88]

Following reports that the U.S. State Department had not found Israel's assurances of following international law credible, [William Hartung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_D._Hartung) wrote that State Department staffers' "desires to align U.S. actions with U.S.and international law have been firmly rebuffed by the Biden administration's leadership team".[^cite_note-89] In August 2024, the [Quincy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quincy_Institute_for_Responsible_Statecraft) published a list of twenty instances in which Israel used U.S. weapons to commit likely war crimes.[^cite_note-90] [Bryan Finucane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryan_Finucane) warned the U.S. could be complicit if Israel used its weapons to commit war crimes.[^cite_note-91]

*[Reuters](18998750.md)* reported that the Biden administration obtained intelligence from Israeli military lawyers that indicated Israel may have committed war crimes. According to the report, the administration continued providing weapons to Israel under the justification that the US had not gathered this information itself.[^cite_note-92] The US also reportedly obtained evidence of Israel's use of Palestinians as [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shields_in_the_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict#Use_by_Israeli_forces).[^cite_note-93]

Pro-Palestinian protester in [Columbus, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio), October 2023

Further information: [Defense for Children International-Palestine et al v. Biden et al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International-Palestine_et_al_v._Biden_et_al)

Some scholars believe that America is [complicit in an alleged genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation#Alleged_international_complicity) committed by Israel against the Palestinians.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96]

In late October 2023, *[The Intercept](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept)* reported that lawyers of the [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) (CCR) warned the Biden administration that they could be held liable "for their failure to prevent Israel's unfolding genocide, as well as for their complicity, by encouraging it and materially supporting it".[^cite_note-97] In November, the Center sued Biden for allegedly failing in his duty under national and international laws to prevent Israel from committing genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-McGreal-2023-98] The CCR argued that "the United States has the means available to have a deterrent effect on Israeli officials now pursuing genocidal acts against the Palestinian people in Gaza."[^cite_note-McGreal-2023-98] In a declaration in the lawsuit, genocide scholar [William Schabas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Schabas) said that in his view there was a "serious risk of genocide" and that the US was "in breach of its obligation" under the [1948 Genocide Convention](143988.md) and international law.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100]

In November 2023, president [Joe Biden](145422.md) was nicknamed "Genocide Joe" by critics of his support for Israel.[^cite_note-Forbes-2023-101] [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib), a Democratic representative of Michigan, accused Biden of supporting "the genocide of the Palestinian people".[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103][^cite_note-Demirjian-2023-104] US Representative [Marjorie Taylor Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Taylor_Greene) ([R](32070.md)) sponsored a resolution to censure Tlaib.[^cite_note-Demirjian-2023-104] National Security Council spokesman [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)), described by Israeli media outlet *[Ynet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ynet)* as "an exceptionally accomplished Israeli advocate,"[^cite_note-105] said "Israel's trying to defend itself against a genocidal terrorist threat. So if we're going to start using that word, fine, let's use it appropriately."[^cite_note-Forbes-2023-101]

Following the [veto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_vetoed_United_Nations_Security_Council_resolutions) of another resolution in the UN Council on 8 December 2023, international human rights organizations issued the following statement: "By continuing to provide Israel with weapons \[and] diplomatic cover as it commits atrocities, including collectively punishing the Palestinian civilian population in Gaza, the US risks complicity in war crimes."[^cite_note-Double_standards-2023-106]

During an August 2024 meeting with humanitarian aid organizations, U.S. diplomat [Lise Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lise_Grande) reportedly responded to concerns about Israel's international humanitarian law violations by responding that Israel was too close an ally for the United States to cease sending weapons.[^cite_note-107] In September 2024, a *[ProPublica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProPublica)* investigation found that [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Agency_for_International_Development) and other agencies found that Israel had deliberately blocked humanitarian aid, but that the U.S. Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) had rejected their findings.[^cite_note-108]

Human Rights Watch (HRW) announced in August 2025 that US military personnel who assist Israeli forces in committing war crimes may be prosecuted for their actions.[^cite_note-109]

### Legal obligations

#### Foreign Assistance Act

On 14 December 2023, Independent Senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) introduced a privileged resolution invoking Section 502(b) of the [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act), calling on the State Department to investigate [Israeli crimes against humanity](75113830.md) in its conduct of the war in Gaza.[^cite_note-110] Sanders said that "This resolution is not prescriptive — it does not alter aid to Israel in any way. It simply requests that the State Department report on how our aid is being used." The resolution would freeze US military aid to Israel unless the State Department issues a report within 30 days.[^cite_note-111] The proposal was defeated, [72 to 11](https://www.senate.gov/legislative/LIS/roll_call_lists/roll_call_vote_cfm.cfm?congress=118\&session=2\&vote=00008), with only Sanders and Democratic Senators [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley), [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen), [Martin Heinrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Heinrich), [Laphonza Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laphonza_Butler), [Ed Markey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Markey), [Ben Ray Luján](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Ray_Luj%C3%A1n), [Mazie Hirono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazie_Hirono), [Peter Welch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Welch) and [Elizabeth Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren) and Republican [Rand Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul) voting for it. Senator [Brian Schatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Schatz) (D-HI) voiced support for the resolution. Among the senators who voted against the resolution were [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) (D-NY), [Mitch McConnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_McConnell) (R-KY), [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) (D-IL), [Bill Cassidy](9386382.md) (R-LA), [Chris Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Murphy) (D-CT), [Rick Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Scott) (R-FL), [Tom Carper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Carper) (D-DE), [Mike Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Braun) (R-IN), [Bob Casey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Casey_Jr.) (D-PA), [Mike Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Lee) (R-UT), and Independent [Angus King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angus_King) of Maine and [Kyrsten Sinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrsten_Sinema) of Arizona.[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] Senator [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons) (D-DE) opposed the resolution. Senator [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin) (D-MD), the chairman of the Foreign Relations Committee, said that the resolution "would be a gift to Hamas, a gift to Iran".[^cite_note-114]

#### Leahy Laws

See also: [Leahy Law § Criticisms of the law and its implementation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leahy_Law#Criticisms_of_the_law_and_its_implementation)

Some Democrats have argued that the \$14.3 billion package the [White House](33057.md) has promised Israel violates the [Leahy Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leahy_Law) because most of the victims of Israel's attacks on [Gaza](12047.md) are civilians. The act forbids the US State and [Defence departments](7279897.md) from providing security support to foreign governments that are suspected of violating [human rights](13831.md). The act's proponents refer to the increasing number of Gaza residents losing their lives as a result of military operations, the forced relocation of over a million people, and the escalating humanitarian situation following Israeli authorities' cuts to the region's supplies of fuel, food, water, and electricity.[^cite_note-Tait-2023-88] A group of top U.S. House Democrats urged the president to enforce the Leahy Laws.[^cite_note-115]

Usamah Andrabi, [Justice Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Democrats)' director of communications – said "I think the Leahy Act should absolutely be looked into right now, when we are seeing gross violations of human rights," he said. "\[The Israelis] are targeting refugee camps, hospitals, mosques all under the guise of self-defense or that one or other member of Hamas is hiding there. It doesn't matter whether Hamas is there or not, because you are targeting civilians. No amount of tax dollars should be justified for that."[^cite_note-Tait-2023-88]

Charles O. Blaha, the former director of the [State Department](31975.md)'s Office of Security and Human Rights, wrote in [Just Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_Security), stating that the U.S. secretary of state's decision not to issue sanctions on the IDF's [Netzah Yehuda Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netzah_Yehuda_Battalion) appeared to be "directly contrary to the Leahy law".[^cite_note-116] Stacy Gilbert, a former-State Department official, stated, "It is widely known and documented in the humanitarian community and the US government that Israel has been blocking humanitarian assistance since the start of the Gaza conflict".[^cite_note-117]

Raed Jarrar, director of advocacy at [DAWN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_for_the_Arab_World_Now), an American nonprofit that fights for democracy and human rights in the Arab world, supported Palestinians who sued the State Department over US aid to the Israeli military, and called on the government to follow the Leahy law.[^cite_note-118]

## Backlash to US support

### Double standards accusations

The [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world) and much of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_North_and_Global_South) accused the United States and its allies of a double standard in condemning [Russia's illegal occupation of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) while supporting [Israel's occupation of the Palestinian territories](2781576.md).[^cite_note-119] Western leaders, pressed for weeks to say whether the loss of thousands of mostly civilian lives could be a violation of international law, spoke only tentatively, adding that they could not judge: "We're not going to get dragged into all this judge-and-jury role," US national security adviser [Jake Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Sullivan) said.[^cite_note-Wintour-2023-120] By comparison, a year earlier the [United States Department of State](31975.md) had officially announced that, based on available information, the US government assessed that members of Russia's forces in Ukraine had committed war crimes.[^cite_note-Wintour-2023-120] The U.S. State Department said there is no need to launch any formal domestic investigation into whether Israel has committed war crimes, even though the weapons it uses are supplied by the US.[^cite_note-Wintour-2023-120] In a speech to the European Parliament, the EU's top diplomat, [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell), said water cuts are a violation of international law regardless of where they occur, whether in [Ukraine](31750.md) or Gaza.[^cite_note-121]

In an interview, [Aaron David Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_David_Miller), a former State Department official, stated to *The New Yorker*, "Do I think that Joe Biden has the same depth of feeling and empathy for the Palestinians of Gaza as he does for the Israelis? No, he doesn't".[^cite_note-122] In April 2024, the Foreign Ministry of Turkey accused the U.S. of having a "double-standard policy on human rights".[^cite_note-123] [Barry Trachtenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Trachtenberg), a professor at [Wake Forest University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wake_Forest_University), criticized Western support of Israeli military actions, stating, "What we're seeing is this clear double-standard where when it's in the interests of the United States and Western allies, they'll invoke international law. When it's not in their interests, they'll clearly violate international law".[^cite_note-124] In July 2024, Spanish prime minister [Pedro Sanchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Sanchez) spoke at the NATO summit in Washington D.C., urging the West to stop its "double standards" between Gaza and Ukraine.[^cite_note-125]

After Israel [killed the American peace activist Ayşenur Ezgi Eygi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Aysenur_Eygi) in the West Bank, another American activist who was shot by Israeli forces wrote an editorial in *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine, stating, "Earlier this year, President Joe Biden said that 'if you harm an American, we will respond.' Yet the Biden Administration has not even condemned my attack."[^cite_note-126] The families of Americans killed by Israel also stated that the Department of Justice had failed to investigate their relatives' deaths.[^cite_note-127] According to a leaked letter, Department of Justice attorneys requested Attorney General [Merrick Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland) to "investigate potential violations of U.S. law by Israel's government, military, and citizenry, and hold the perpetrators to account."[^cite_note-128]

### Criticisms by human rights organisations, international organizations and NGOs

Human rights organizations and UN officials have heavily criticized the Biden administration for vetoing multiple UN resolutions calling for an immediate ceasefire and for continuing to send arms to Israel. Palestinian president [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) said the US was complicit in war crimes,[^cite_note-129] and Amnesty International's Secretary General said that the veto showed that the US "displays a callous disregard for civilian suffering in the face of a staggering death toll".[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-2023-130] [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) said that the veto "stands in sharp contrast to the values it professes to uphold," and that the US was providing "diplomatic cover for the ongoing atrocities in Gaza".[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-2023-130] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) said that the veto and the military support "risks complicity in war crimes".[^cite_note-Lederer-2023-7] Following the veto, a UN General Assembly resolution calling for a ceasefire garnered overwhelming support, with 153 countries voting for the resolution to 10 opposed.[^cite_note-131] Turkey's president called for reform of the UN Security Council system that allows for the five permanent members to veto resolutions supported by the overwhelming majority of countries.[^cite_note-132]

### Criticism in the Middle East

Polling by the [Arab Center for Research and Policy Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Center_for_Research_and_Policy_Studies) showed 94 percent of respondents in sixteen Middle East countries had a negative view of U.S. policy in the war and 76 percent had a diminished view of the country due to its policies.[^cite_note-133] In a social media post, the [Palestine UN mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Ambassador_to_the_United_Nations) criticized the US secretary of state for not acknowledging the tens of thousands of killed Palestinians in his post marking the 100 days since the start of the war.[^cite_note-134]

### Criticism by other international politicians

On 4 February 2024, Irish MEP [Mick Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mick_Wallace) accused the United States of lacking respect for other cultures or international law after it initiated a bombing campaign against Yemen.[^cite_note-135]

Following a US veto of a UN Security Council resolution calling for an immediate ceasefire, [Zhang Jun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Jun_\(ambassador\)), China's ambassador to the UN, stated it was "nothing different from giving the green light to the continued slaughter".[^cite_note-136]

### Within Congress

#### U.S. House



Israeli prime minister Netanyahu addresses a [joint session of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_session_of_the_United_States_Congress) with House Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) and Senator [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin), 24 July 2024.

On 29 October 2023, the only Palestinian-American in Congress, [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib) (D-MI), accused Israel of [committing genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza), saying, "President Biden, not all America is with you on this one, and you need to wake up and understand. We are literally watching people commit genocide."[^cite_note-Smith-2023-137] Representative [Lloyd Doggett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Doggett) (D-TX) stated on 27 December 2023, that U.S. policy in Israel had failed.[^cite_note-138] Representative [Jim McGovern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_McGovern_\(American_politician\)) (D-MA) stated on 3 January 2024, that Israeli officials' statements calling for the forced displacement of Palestinians from Gaza were "reprehensible".[^cite_note-139] Tlaib and Congressman [Cori Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cori_Bush) (D-MO) released a joint statement on 12 January in support of the [*South Africa* International Court of Justice case](75736183.md), stating, "The US must stop trying to discredit and undermine this case and the international legal system it claims to support."[^cite_note-140] Congressmembers [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna) (D-CA) and [Val Hoyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val_Hoyle) (D-OR) and Senator [Mike Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Lee) (R-UT) condemned Biden's [2024 missile strikes in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_missile_strikes_in_Yemen) on 12 January, stating that only Congress has the power to approve a war.[^cite_note-141] A 19 January letter from 60 Democratic Congressmembers urged U.S. secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) to firmly condemn the forced displacement of Palestinians from Gaza.[^cite_note-142] A group of a dozen Jewish Congressmembers issued a statement on 19 January condemning Netanyahu's opposition to a Palestinian state, which read, "We strongly disagree with the Prime Minister. A two-state solution is the path forward."[^cite_note-143]

Texas Congressman Lloyd Doggett wrote on 24 January: "After all America has done for him, if Netanyahu 'needs to be able to say no' to us, we need to say no to him and do so now!"[^cite_note-144] On 24 January, a group of five Congressmembers requested the [Government Accountability Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Accountability_Office) to review whether arms transferred to Israel were being used to violate international law.[^cite_note-145] [Mark Pocan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Pocan) (D-WI) criticized Biden's decision to [suspend funding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_October_7_controversy) to [UNRWA](32270.md), stating on 29 January: "UNRWA feeds 1.2 million people a day, as well as helps distribute aid now to all Palestinians in need. Aid needs to be restored now to help the displaced millions in Gaza."[^cite_note-146] [Chuy Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuy_Garcia) (D-IL) stated that the "decision to freeze UNRWA funding" should be reversed.[^cite_note-147] On 11 February 2024, Congresswoman Cori Bush criticized Israel's [impending invasion of Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), stating, "Almost half of Gaza's population has taken refuge in Rafah. There's nowhere else to go."[^cite_note-148] Congressman Ro Khanna also criticized Biden's response to Israel's planned attack on [Rafah](584373.md), stating, "This is not the time for vague generalities about doing more to protect civilian life."[^cite_note-149]

On 28 February, U.S. congresswoman [Madeleine Dean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madeleine_Dean) (D-PA) called for an end to the war and condemned Netanyahu, stating, "Prime Minister Netanyahu's dishonesty – his broken promises to use precision strikes to protect civilians and his false claims that safe zones have been created and enforced – is unacceptable".[^cite_note-150] A group of two dozen House members demanded Biden and Blinken take steps to ensure [journalists' safety](75146557.md) in Gaza, stating: "Not enough steps have been taken to safeguard the lives of the civilian population in Gaza, including journalists".[^cite_note-151] On 29 February, Congressman Jim McGovern called for the U.S. to increase [humanitarian aid to Gaza](76066918.md), stating, "Massive humanitarian support is needed to save innocent lives."[^cite_note-152] A group of six U.S. House representatives – [Rosa DeLauro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa_DeLauro) (D-CT), [Sean Casten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Casten) (D-IL), Madeleine Dean, [Becca Balint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Becca_Balint) (D-VT), [Salud Carbajal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salud_Carbajal) (D-CA), and [Mark Takano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Takano) (D-CA) – returned from a trip to Israel and released a joint statement, saying, "We are deeply worried that Prime Minister Netanyahu is moving toward the total destruction of Gaza and has demonstrated an utter disregard for Palestinian lives".[^cite_note-153] [Bonnie Watson Coleman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonnie_Watson_Coleman) (D-NJ) stated in May 2024, "We are way past the red line. We cannot continue supporting this."[^cite_note-154]

In October 2024, a group of several dozen House Democrats signed a letter to the Biden Administration, urging tougher accountability regarding press access to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-155] In November 2025, Democratic Representative Rashida Tlaib, with the support of 20 other Democratic members of Congress and more than 100 human rights groups, has introduced a resolution recognizing Israel's assault on Gaza as genocide.[^cite_note-156]

#### U.S. Senate



Senate Majority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) with Israeli President Herzog in Tel Aviv, Israel, 15 October 2023

On 2 November 2023, Senator [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) (D-IL) became the first U.S. senator to call for a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-157] He was followed by [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley) (D-OR) later that same month.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] On 23 December 2023, US senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) (D-MD), stated, "There's a big gap between what the United States says is essential, and what the Netanyahu government is prepared to do. And when you see these big gaps, the United States looks feckless."[^cite_note-160] US senator [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine) (D-VA) criticized Biden's emergency sale of weapons to Israel on 30 December 2023, stating, "Why should the Admin bypass Congress on arms sales to any nation? Bypassing Congress = keeping the American public in the dark."[^cite_note-161] U.S. senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) (I-VT) forced a vote on 17 January 2024, on a resolution requiring the State Department to explore whether U.S. weapons were being used to violate international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-162]

On 7 February 2024, U.S. senator [Chris Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Murphy) (D-CT) stated he would support an amendment requiring weapons sold internationally to be used in compliance with U.S. law, [international humanitarian law](1093604.md), and the laws of armed conflict.[^cite_note-163] In a speech on 13 February, Senator Chris Van Hollen accused the Israeli government of [committing war crimes](75113830.md) in Gaza, stating, "Kids in Gaza are now dying from the deliberate withholding of food. That is a war crime. It is a textbook war crime. And that makes those who orchestrate it war criminals."[^cite_note-164] On 9 March, Senator Bernie Sanders criticized members of Congress, stating, "It is absurd to criticise Netanyahu's war in one breath and provide another \$10bn to continue that war in the next."[^cite_note-165] On 29 April, Senator Van Hollen of Maryland there was "serious doubt on the integrity of the process in the Biden administration for reviewing whether the Netanyahu government is complying with international law in Gaza".[^cite_note-166]

In May 2024, Bernie Sanders asked how the U.S. would be able to criticize other countries' human rights abuses if it ignored the "crimes against humanity" committed in Gaza.[^cite_note-167] In June 2024, Senator Chris Van Hollen urged sanctions against Israeli finance minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) during a U.S. congressional subcommittee meeting.[^cite_note-168] In September 2025, Senators Van Hollen and [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley), after returning from a congressional delegation trip to the Middle East, said that Israel was operating under a systematic plan to destroy and ethnically cleanse Palestinians from Gaza in order to force the local population to leave, and they said that the United States was complicit.[^cite_note-169] In September 2025, Bernie Sanders became the first US senator to say that Israel was committing genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-170]

In September 2026, Sen. [Jeanne Shaheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanne_Shaheen), the top Democrat on the [Senate Foreign Relations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Foreign_Relations), placed a hold on a \$2.8 billion weapons sale to Israel that included 40,000 one-ton bombs. Shaheen cited Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu's failure to address concerns over civilian deaths in Gaza, Israeli strikes in Syria, and settler violence against Palestinians in the West Bank.[^cite_note-171]

### Within the Biden administration

In January 2024, a letter was signed by more than a dozen Biden campaign staffers calling for condition of US support to Israel and a ceasefire. Paul told [The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian) that the administration is witnessing "pretty extraordinary levels of dissent".[^cite_note-172] On 2 February 2024, a group of 800 U.S. and European officials signed an open letter stating their governments' policies were weakening their nations' "moral standing".[^cite_note-173] Following the [World Central Kitchen drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes) on 1 April 2024, internal dissent within the Biden administration on the issue of weapons sales to Israel increased.[^cite_note-174]

During a February 2024 meeting between U.S. diplomats, humanitarian workers, and [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) director [Samantha Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Power), staff were reportedly "visibly upset" with the administration's policy, with some wearing [keffiyehs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keffiyeh) in protest and others breaking down into tears.[^cite_note-175]

[Jake Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Sullivan), former national security advisor to Biden, admitted in an interview published in January 2025 that he had doubts about whether the Biden administration had done the right thing in continuing supporting Israel with arms because of the "immense human suffering in Gaza".[^cite_note-176]

#### Resignations

By 7 January 2024, two officials from the Biden administration—Josh Paul and Tariq Habash, working on arms sale at the State Department and policy adviser at the Department of Education respectively—had resigned in opposition to US support to Israel's war efforts.[^cite_note-177] In March 2024, another State Department staffer working on human rights, Anelle Sheline, resigned over the administration's support to the war, in particular its bypassing of Congress to authorize the sale of weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-178] In April 2024, the U.S. State Department Arabic spokesperson resigned in protest of the administration's Gaza policy.[^cite_note-179] In May 2024, [Lily Greenberg Call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lily_Greenberg_Call), the special assistant to the Interior Department's chief of staff, resigned in protest of Biden's Gaza policies.[^cite_note-180] On 30 May, a senior State Department official resigned, stating U.S. statements that Israel was not blocking humanitarian aid into Gaza were false.[^cite_note-181] In June 2024, the twelve public Biden administration resignees signed a joint statement calling Biden's Gaza policy "a failure and a threat to U.S. national security".[^cite_note-182] In July 2024, a political appointee in the Department of the Interior resigned in protest of Biden's Gaza policies.[^cite_note-183]

### In the U.S. military

On 25 February 2024, [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Bushnell), a 25-year-old serviceman of the [United States Air Force](32090.md), died after [setting himself on fire](916669.md) outside the front gate of the [Embassy of Israel in Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_Washington,_D.C.), declaring that he would "no longer be [complicit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide) in [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza)".[^cite_note-184]

In May 2024, US Army Major Harrison Mann resigned in protest of Biden's Gaza policies.[^cite_note-185] In June 2024, an Air Force engineer resigned in protest of the United States' "complicit\[ity] in the genocide in Gaza".[^cite_note-186] Later the same month, two U.S. Air Force enlistees stated they were seeking [conscientious objector status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscientious_objection_in_the_United_States) due to the United States' support for Israel's action in Gaza.[^cite_note-187]

### Muslim and Arab Americans

Amongst the [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_States) and [Arab American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_American) communities, there was strong backlash to US support of Israel, with some vowing to abstain from supporting Biden in the 2024 presidential election.[^cite_note-188] [Palestinian-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian-American) with family in Gaza were reportedly "pleading with the U.S. government to evacuate their family members".[^cite_note-189] Some, including former-Congressman [Justin Amash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Amash), reported the death of family members from Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-190] American citizens stuck in Gaza also criticized the United States government's response to the conflict.[^cite_note-191] In a meeting with Biden's campaign chair [Julie Chávez Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Ch%C3%A1vez_Rodriguez), the publisher of *[Arab American News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_American_News)* reportedly told her, "If this man wants our vote, he has to do more than Jesus Christ – bring a lot more dead back to life. Thousands of people's blood is on his hands."[^cite_note-192] In response to late-February 2024 reports that Biden again planned to veto a UN resolution for a ceasefire, the [US Campaign for Palestinian Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Campaign_for_Palestinian_Rights) stated, "Our government is beyond evil."[^cite_note-193]

During the [Michigan Democratic primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Michigan_Democratic_presidential_primary), Biden [received less than a quarter of the vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protest_vote_movements) in predominantly Arab areas in [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan).[^cite_note-194] In 2024, Dearborn Mayor [Abdullah Hammoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_Hammoud) declined an invitation to meet with Biden campaign officials prior to the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md), due to the Biden administration's stance on the Israel-Hamas war.[^cite_note-195] On [Super Tuesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Tuesday#2024), nearly 20% of voters in Minnesota voted [uncommitted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncommitted_\(voting_option\)) in protest of Biden's Gaza policies.[^cite_note-196] In March 2024, a coalition of Muslim and Palestinian organizations from Chicago refused a meeting with the Biden campaign, stating, "There is no point in more meetings".[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198]

### Internal public opinion



Gallup poll showing US change from majority approval (Nov. 2023) to majority disapproval (March 2024) of Israeli military action in Gaza

[^cite_note-Jones-2024a-199]

In a [Reuters](18998750.md)/[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll released on 15 November 2023, 32% of respondents said that the U.S. should support Israel, 39% said that the U.S. should be a neutral mediator, 4% said that the U.S. should support Palestinians, and 15% said the U.S. shouldn't be involved at all.[^cite_note-200]

In a [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) poll released on 30 November 2023, 50% of the U.S. respondents approved of Israel's military action in Gaza, and 45% disapproved.[^cite_note-201] In November 2023, the Jewish Election Institute found that 74% of [Jewish American](1131183.md) registered voters approved of the Biden administration's handling of the war.[^cite_note-202]

In a [Data for Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_for_Progress) poll released on 5 December 2023, 61% of likely voters, including a majority of Democrats (76%) and Independents (57%) and a plurality of Republicans (49%), supported the U.S. calling for a permanent ceasefire and a de-escalation of violence in Gaza; 83% of Democrats, 74% of Independents, and 63% of Republicans supported sending food, water, and medical supplies to people in Gaza; 77% of Democrats, 63% of Independents, and 55% of Republicans supported ensuring that people in Gaza have reliable access to fuel and electricity; and 63% of voters, including 65% of those under age 45, agreed with the statement that "The U.S. should hold its ally Israel to a high standard and only provide military aid to Israel if they meet our standards for human rights."[^cite_note-203]

In a *[New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times)*/[Siena College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siena_College_Research_Institute) poll released on 19 December 2023, 44 percent of voters said that Israel should stop its military campaign to protect against civilian casualties, and 39 percent said that Israel should continue its military campaign even if it means that civilian casualties in Gaza mount. 57% of voters said that they disapproved of President Biden's handling of the Israeli-Palestinian conflict, whereas 33% approved.[^cite_note-204]

In an *[Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*/[YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll released on 24 January 2024, 35% of U.S. adults agreed that Israel's military campaign against Palestinians amounts to [genocide](12441.md), 36% disagreed, and 29% were undecided. Among U.S. citizens aged 18–29, 49% of those surveyed agreed that Israel is committing genocide, with 24% disagreeing, and 27% uncertain.[^cite_note-205]

In an [Associated Press](18935732.md)-NORC Center for Public Affairs Research poll released on 2 February 2024, 50% of U.S. adults said that the military response from Israel in the Gaza Strip had gone too far, whereas 31% said that it had "been about right", and 15% thought that it had "not gone far enough".[^cite_note-206]

As of 25 February 2024, over 70 cities in the United States have voted for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208]

In a Gallup poll released on 4 March 2024, positive opinions of Israel had dropped from 64% to 38% amongst young people aged 18 to 34. Overall, 58% of Americans had a positive view of Israel.[^cite_note-Jones-2024b-209]

In a [Center for Economic and Policy Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Economic_and_Policy_Research) poll released on 5 March 2024, 52% of U.S. citizens supported ending arms transfers to Israel. 62% of Democrats supported halting arms sales, while 14% disagreed, whereas 30% of Republicans supported the measure, and 55% opposed it.[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-210][^cite_note-CEPR-2024-211]

In a Gallup poll released on 27 March 2024, 55% of the responders disapproved of Israel's military action in Gaza, 36% approved, and 9% had no opinion.[^cite_note-Jones-2024a-199]

In a [Data for Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_for_Progress) poll, in collaboration with [Zeteo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeteo), released on 8 May 2024, 83% of Democrats, 56% of Republicans, and 70% of all likely voters supported the United States calling for a permanent ceasefire a de-escalation of violence in Gaza; 56% of Democrats, 23% of Republicans, and 39% of all likely voters believed that Israel is committing genocide against Palestinians in Gaza, with 22% of Democrats, 19% of Republicans, and 23% of all likely voters being uncertain regarding the issue; and 55% of Democrats, 36% of Republicans, and 46% of all likely voters said that they disapprove of college campuses limiting [students' rights and abilities to protest against Israel's military operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses), whereas 32% of Democrats, 49% of Republicans, and 40% of all likely voters approved of doing so.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216]

A [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll released on 21 May 2024, and conducted in the key swing states [Arizona](21883824.md), [Michigan](18859.md), [Minnesota](19590.md), [Pennsylvania](23332.md), and [Wisconsin](33127.md), with a highly representative sample of 500 registered Democratic and Independent voters, found that between 74.1% and 82.8% of the respondents supported an immediate and permanent ceasefire, depending on the state, and that between 19.2% and 24.2% of the respondents are less likely to vote for Biden in the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md) because of his handling of the war in Gaza. Over 40% of the polled voters in each state stated that the combination of imposing a lasting ceasefire and conditioning aid to Israel would make them more likely to vote for Biden.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]

A [CBS News](359551.md) poll released on 9 June 2024, found that 61 percent of all Americans think that the United States should not send weapons and supplies to Israel, including 77 percent of Democrats and nearly 40 percent of Republicans.[^cite_note-219]

According to a Data for Progress poll released on 12 June 2024, 64% of all likely voters, including 86% of Democrats, 64% of Independents, 62% of swing voters, and 43% of Republicans supported Joe Biden's proposed ceasefire deal in Gaza, with the specifics: "A complete ceasefire and withdrawal of Israeli forces from Gaza. The release of Israeli hostages in exchange for hundreds of Palestinian prisoners. A surge of humanitarian aid into Gaza. Allowing displaced Palestinians to return to their homes in northern Gaza and starting a 3-5 year reconstruction plan for the region, backed by the United States, Europe, and international institutions". The survey also found a 53% sum total majority support for withdrawing military aid from Israel if it rejected the ceasefire proposal, including 70% of Democrats, 51% of Independents, 53% of swing voters, and 35% of Republicans, with 36% being against it.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]

According to a [Chicago Council on Global Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Council_on_Global_Affairs) poll released on 6 August 2024, and conducted online between 21 June and 1 July 55% of all sum total respondents oppose using US troops to defend Israel if it is attacked by a neighboring country, whereas 41% support it.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]

According to a Pew Research in 2025, more than half of American adults (53%) express an unfavorable opinion of Israel, compared to 42% in March 2022 - before 7 October 2023.[^cite_note-225] After nearly two years of war, the American people support the Palestinians against Israel for the first time.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

According to a Washington Post poll from October 2025, 61% of US Jews believe that Israel has committed war crimes in Gaza, while 39% believe that Israel is committing genocide.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]

An [Associated Press](18935732.md)-NORC Center for Public Affairs Research poll conducted in June 2026 found that 58% of Democrats believed the US was too supportive of Israel, compared with 45% in January 2024. The poll also found that a majority of Democrats believed the US was not providing enough support to Palestinians and highlighted growing partisan differences over U.S. policy toward Israel and the Gaza war.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231]

### Israeli officials

Some Israeli government members believed that Biden's support for Israel was not strong enough. In an interview with the *[Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_Journal)*, Israel's Minister of National Security [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md) stated, "Instead of giving us his full backing, Biden is busy with giving humanitarian aid and fuel \[to Gaza], which goes to Hamas. If Trump was in power, the U.S. conduct would be completely different."[^cite_note-232] Israel's Minister of Finance [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) stated Biden was involved in an anti-Semitic lie for issuing sanctions against violent West Bank settlers.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-235] Following comments by Biden in March 2024 that Israel's killing of civilians was "hurting Israel more than helping", the Israeli prime minister Netanyahu stated this was "false" and "wrong".[^cite_note-236] After a speech by Chuck Schumer calling for new elections after the war, [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) released a statement, saying, "Senator Schumer is expected to respect Israel's elected government and not undermine it. This is always true and even more so in wartime."[^cite_note-237]

On 27 March, Ben-Gvir stated, "Biden prefers the line of Rashida Tlaib and Sinwar to the line of Benjamin Netanyahu and Ben-Gvir. I would have expected the president of the United States not to take their line, but rather to take ours."[^cite_note-238] Following reports the U.S. was considering sanctions against an IDF unit charged with human rights abuses, the Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu called the sanctions the "height of absurdity and a moral low".[^cite_note-239] Diaspora Minister [Amichai Chikli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amichai_Chikli) stated, "If I were an American citizen with the right to vote, I'd vote for Trump and Republicans".[^cite_note-240] In response to the Biden administration stating it was considering halting weapons transfers to Israel for its [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), Ben-Gvir tweeted, "Hamas loves Biden".[^cite_note-241] In June 2024, Netanyahu criticized the Biden administration for what he called a "dramatic drop" in weapons deliveries.[^cite_note-242]

Senior Israeli officials stated that Donald Trump's campaign promise to quickly end the war in Gaza would cause challenges for the Israeli government if he were to win the 2024 election, due to Israel's "internal political constraints to ending the war quickly".[^cite_note-243]

### Inside the U.S.

See also: [Gaza war protests in the United States](75347645.md) and [List of U.S. officials who resigned over Biden's support for Israel in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._officials_who_resigned_over_Biden's_support_for_Israel_in_the_Gaza_war)

#### October reactions



Pro-Palestinian protest outside [Sproul Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sproul_Plaza) at the [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley), 25 October 2023

- Eva Borgwardt, the political director of [IfNotNow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IfNotNow), a progressive Jewish organization, stated: "The White House and many in the US government are clear as they should be that 1,000 Israelis killed is too many, Our question for them is: How many Palestinian deaths are too many?"

  [^cite_note-Gambino-2023-244]

- [Alexandra Rojas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandra_Rojas), the executive director of [Justice Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Democrats), begged the president to take immediate action to stop a ground invasion that would "ensure thousands more civilian casualties, bring us closer to an all-out regional conflict in the Middle East, and thrust the United States into another endless war" in a statement released amid increasing bombing and a communications blackout in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-Gambino-2023-244]

- A [Quinnipiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_University) study done following the Gaza war revealed that 76 percent of voters believed supporting Israel is in the national interest of the United States, and 64 percent approved of sending weapons and military equipment to Israel. This support was more pronounced among older age groups, with 51% of those under 35 disapproving of this action.

  [^cite_note-Gambino-2023-244]

  [^cite_note-245]

- An [NPR](29697232.md)/*[PBS NewsHour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS_NewsHour)*/[Marist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marist_College) poll showed that 48% of [millennials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennials) and members of [Generation Z](4015968.md) thought that the US should voice support for Israel.

  [^cite_note-Smith-2023-137]

- Democrats' opinion of Biden's job dropped by 11 percent during October 2023. According to Gallup, Biden alienated some members of his own party with his swift and decisive show of support for Israel.

  [^cite_note-Smith-2023-137]

- US [Republican](32070.md) Congressman and former aide to [Donald Trump](4848272.md), [Max Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Miller_\(politician\)), speaking at [Fox News](11121.md) stated that Palestine is "about to get eviscerated ... to turn that into a parking lot". He previously called on the Biden administration "to get out of Israel's way and to let Israel do what it needs to do best" and said there should be "no rules of engagement" during Israel's bombardment of Gaza.

  [^cite_note-246]

  &#x20;Miller also questioned the accuracy of the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md)'s claim that 10,000 people had been killed in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-247]

#### November reactions



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Los Angeles](18110.md) against the war in Gaza and Hollywood's role in [dehumanizing Muslims](162173.md), November 2023

- On 1 November 2023, the Republican representative, [Brian Mast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Mast), compared all Palestinians to Nazis on the [House](19468510.md) floor.

  [^cite_note-248]

- A team of legal experts alerted the Biden administration and the ICC prosecutor that the U.S. government might be legally implicated in Israel's ongoing war against the Palestinians.

  [^cite_note-Speri-2023-30]

- The executive director of [DAWN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_for_the_Arab_World_Now), [Sarah Leah Whitson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Leah_Whitson), stated that Biden's funding request "isn't just giving a green light for ethnic cleansing—it's bankrolling it."

  [^cite_note-249]

- [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) in an interview emphasized "nobody's hands are clean."

  [^cite_note-250]

- The [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) (CCR) sued Joe Biden and two of his cabinet colleagues (On behalf of Palestinian human rights organizations, Palestinians living in Gaza, and US citizens with family in the besieged region) for their failure to stop and aiding and abetting "genocide" in Gaza. US citizen Laila al-Haddad, one of the case's plaintiffs (who has lost five relatives in Gaza as a result of Israel's attacks) said: "I paid for Israel to kill my cousins and my aunt, there's no two ways around it, It was my tax dollars that did that."

  [^cite_note-251]

- In November 2023, [Bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden)'s "*Letter to the American people*," published in 2002, went viral on [TikTok](56822861.md) and other social media. In the letter, Bin Laden denounced numerous acts of American aggression such as U.S. support for Israel, and supported [al-Qaeda](1921.md)'s war against the U.S. as a defensive struggle. Numerous social media users, including Americans, expressed their opposition to US foreign policy by widely sharing the letter and its contents. The letter was removed from *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* website after more than 20 years of being present online in the news outlet's webpage, and TikTok began issuing takedowns of videos featuring the letter.

  [^cite_note-252]

  [^cite_note-253]

  [^cite_note-254]

  &#x20;Reporting in *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* suggested that the virality of the letter had been limited prior to media coverage, having never trended on TikTok, that many of the TikTok videos covering the letter were critical of bin Laden, and that the media coverage had exaggerated its significance and elevated the virality of the letter.

  [^cite_note-255]

- [Angie Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angie_Nixon) ([D](5043544.md)), a member of the [Florida House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida_House_of_Representatives), sponsored a resolution calling for "de-escalation" and a ceasefire. The resolution was rejected by a vote of 104 to 2, but there was controversy during the legislative debate. Nixon said: "We are at 10,000 dead Palestinians. How many will be enough?" State Representative [Michelle Salzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Salzman) ([R](32070.md)) replied, "All of them." Some commentators called Salzman's remark a call for genocide. Nixon and the Florida chapter of the [Council on American–Islamic Relations](159138.md) called for Salzman to be censured or resign.

  [^cite_note-256]

  [^cite_note-257]

  &#x20;The executive director of the [US Campaign for Palestinian Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Campaign_for_Palestinian_Rights) (USCPR), Ahmad Abuznaid, said, "There is a bipartisan effort to dehumanize the Palestinian people".

  [^cite_note-258]

- In March of 2024, The *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)* reported that in November of 2023, U.S. Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) had received a classified briefing on the [Al-Shifa Hospital Siege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_Hospital_Siege) and told the paper that there were "important and subtle differences" between what the Biden administration was saying and the actual intelligence on al-Shifa. The Biden administration denied the Senator's statements.

  [^cite_note-259]

#### December reactions

- A 19 December [New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times)/[Siena College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siena_College) poll found 57 percent of Americans disapproved of Joe Biden's handling of the conflict, with a plurality supporting Israel ending its military actions to protect civilians. Three quarters of 18-to-29 year olds disapproved of Biden's handling, with half saying Israel was intentionally killing civilians and three-fourths saying it was not taking enough precautions for civilians.

  [^cite_note-260]

- Polling conducted by [Shibley Telhami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shibley_Telhami) at the [University of Maryland, College Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Maryland,_College_Park) showed the number of Democrats under 35 less likely to vote for Biden due to the war had grown to 21 percent in just a two-week period.

  [^cite_note-261]

- Former Republican [Representative](19468510.md) [Michele Bachmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michele_Bachmann) appearing in [The Charlie Kirk Show](53245776.md) stated "So, it's time that Gaza ends. The two million people who live there – they are clever assassins. They need to be removed from that land. That land needs to be turned into a national park. And since they're the voluntary mercenaries for Iran, they need to be dropped on the doorstep of Iran. Let Iran deal with those people." She received a round of applause from the audience, while Kirk replied "I look at Israel and Israel says we never want another person into our country that doesn't share our values," Kirk said. "They said they don't want refugees. They don't want any of these people. I want American immigration policy to be like that."

  [^cite_note-262]

  [^cite_note-263]

#### January reactions



[March on Washington for Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_on_Washington_for_Gaza) in January 2024

- Tariq Habash, a policy adviser at the [US Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Department_of_Education) resigned, stating, "I cannot represent an administration that systematically dehumanizes Palestinians and enables their ethnic cleansing."

  [^cite_note-264]

- [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) political scientist [Stephen Walt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Walt) stated, "Biden looks heartless or clueless (or both) and many see him as no better than Trump."

  [^cite_note-265]

- The [Center for Civilians in Conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Civilians_in_Conflict) called the failure of Sanders' resolution to investigate Israeli compliance with international law by 72-to-11 "deeply disappointing".

  [^cite_note-266]

- [Aaron David Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_David_Miller), a former-State Department official, stated Joe Biden's policies had turned the United States into "Israel's lawyer".

  [^cite_note-267]

- According to the poll conducted by the [Harvard CAPS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_American_Political_Studies)–[Harris Poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Harris_Poll) on 17–18 January 2024, 67% of American respondents said that a ceasefire in Gaza should only happen after all Israeli hostages are released and Hamas is removed from power. 74% of American respondents believed that the Hamas attack was [genocidal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel), while 34% of respondents believed that Israel was committing [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation).

  [^cite_note-268]

- An *[Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*/[YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll found 49 percent of 18-29-year-olds agreed with the statement that Israel is committing genocide, and 49 percent of registered Democrats also agreeing that Israel was committing genocide.

  [^cite_note-269]

- In an op-ed, Bernie Sanders wrote, "The United States must stop asking Israel to do the right thing. It's time to start telling Israel it must do these things or it will lose our support."

  [^cite_note-270]

- The [Arizona Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arizona_Democratic_Party) passed a resolution calling for a ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-271]

- The cities of [San Francisco](49728.md) and [Chicago](6886.md) passed resolutions calling for a ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-272]

  [^cite_note-273]
