## Analysis

U.S. lawmakers have long viewed Israel as an ally to help protect U.S. strategic interests in the Middle East. Maintaining Israel's regional military hegemony, according to Al-Jazeera, is a central element of U.S. Middle East policy. This has been achieved with U.S. financial aid and an increase in Israel's military arsenal.[^cite_note-How_big_is_Israel-2023-31] On the question of why Joe Biden is reluctant to freeze arms transfers or impose conditions on future supplies, Guardian journalist, Robert Tait writes that the US is concerned that pressure on [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) could push him towards a common goal with [Republicans](32070.md) in a year leading to the election.[^cite_note-332] Josh Paul, who resigned over sending arms to Israel, has described Biden policy in favor of "the status quo of the occupation" and a "shortsighted, destructive, unjust" policy that "will only lead to more and deeper suffering for both the Israeli and the Palestinian people — and is not in the long term American interest."[^cite_note-333]

Although the U.S. has asked Israel to protect civilians and allow humanitarian aid, State Secretary Antony Blinken acknowledged that there is "a gap" between these appeals and "the actual results that we're seeing on the ground". The United States has not used its leverage over Israel—its weapons transfers and diplomatic support—to ensure civilian safety, leading the *Washington Post* to describe American rhetoric as a "good cop-bad cop approach". Aaron David Miller of the [CEIP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_Endowment_for_International_Peace) said that the Biden administration deserved credit for the humanitarian pause negotiations, but that it had "tethered" itself to Israeli war aims that cannot be achieved without bringing "grievous harm to the civilian population".[^cite_note-334]

[Ali Harb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Harb) described the contradictions between media reports about Biden's "frustration" with Netanyahu, and his continued military support for Israel, stating, "The US keeps calling for minimising civilian casualties, but the Palestinian death toll keeps growing. The Biden administration repeatedly emphasises the two-state solution; Israeli leaders continue to explicitly say they oppose the establishment of a Palestinian state."[^cite_note-335] In March 2024, [Al Jazeera English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) senior analyst [Marwan Bishara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_Bishara) assessed a perceived shift in Biden's tone toward the war, stating, "Biden is underlining a change of tone, not a change of policy. And that explains a bit why he sounds as if he's speaking from both sides of his mouth."[^cite_note-336]

In late-March 2024, Tamer Qarmout, a professor at the [Doha Institute for Graduate Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha_Institute_for_Graduate_Studies), stated, "You don't see the US using its real leverage to stop Israel or correct its behaviours."[^cite_note-337] While Republican support for Israel remains strong, many Democrats are debating making future U.S. military aid conditional on Israel's behavior in the West Bank and Gaza. According to [Steven A. Cook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_A._Cook), for the United States, a more normal bilateral relationship with Israel is likely to reduce the moral costs of military aid.[^cite_note-338] In an op-ed, Elias Khoury argued that the United States's support of Israel "has eroded its international authority and claim to uphold the international rules-based system".[^cite_note-339]

Amidst criticism that Biden's blanket support for Israel's actions had weakened the supposed [rules-based order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rules-based_order), Lara Friedman of the Foundation for Middle East Peace, wrote, "The costs of these new rules of war will be paid with the blood of civilians worldwide for generations to come, and the U.S. responsibility for enabling, defending, and normalizing these new rules, and their horrific, dehumanizing consequences will not be forgotten."[^cite_note-340]

Senator Bernie Sanders attributed Washington's reluctance to alter its stance on backing Israel to the financial influence of lobbying groups. "If you speak up on that issue, you'll have super Pacs like Aipac going after you," Sanders said.[^cite_note-341]

### U.S. isolation

Israel and the United States have become increasingly isolated amid growing global calls for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-Time-2023-1][^cite_note-AP_News-2023-2][^cite_note-The_Guardian-2023-3][^cite_note-NBC_News-2023-342] American political scientist [Ian Bremmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Bremmer) stated that the [Biden administration](65732174.md)'s position unconditionally supporting the [Israeli invasion of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) has left Joe Biden as isolated on the world stage as Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) has been since the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) began in 2022.[^cite_note-343] [Jeffrey Sachs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Sachs), a professor of economics at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University), also stated that the United States was being isolated on the world stage due to its support for Israel.[^cite_note-344] In March 2024, U.S. senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) wrote the U.S. was "virtually alone in the world" in continuing to defend Israel's actions as the Palestinian death toll has sparked [widespread outrage and protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Palestine_protests_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-345]

### Peace process

According to some analyses, the US-led ceasefire talks are simply buying more time for Israel's war in Gaza to continue.[^cite_note-346] Following US request to Qatar to expel Hamas from the country, according to the BBC, this is the result of the Biden administration's less influence over Israel, which led them to believe that the best way to force some sort of deal was by putting pressure on Hamas.[^cite_note-347] Writing in *[Le Monde Diplomatique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde_diplomatique)*, Adam Shatz said that Israel had taken advantage of the United States' facilitation of a peace process to assassinate [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) and [Hasan Nasrallah](313436.md), writing that "Netanyahu helped the Americans to draft a ceasefire proposal he had no intention of honouring, while conspiring to kill the Arab leaders with whom the ceasefire was to be reached."[^cite_note-348]

## Timeline

### 2023

#### October

- Hours after the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md) on 7 October, the United States started sending warships and warplanes into the region, prepared to give Israel whatever it needs.

  [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

  - US secretary of defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) ordered the deployment of [United States Navy](20518076.md)'s [Carrier Strike Group 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Strike_Group_12), led by the [USS Gerald R. Ford](4050542.md).

    [^cite_note-349]

    [^cite_note-350]

    [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

  - Israel asked the United States for Iron Dome interceptors, and President Joe Biden said Washington would quickly provide additional equipment and resources, including ammunition, which are going to reach Israel within days.

  - US secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) said Washington would provide its "full support" to Israel, with guided missile launchers and F-35 fighter jets among the equipment being sent.

    [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

- By 10 October, more ships and troops were on their way to Israel, and additional troops in the United States were being prepared to deploy if requested. One US aircraft carrier and its strike group were already in the eastern Mediterranean, and a second US carrier had left and was underway.&#x20;

  - In addition, three naval warships were sent to the area. A large number of aircraft were sent to US military bases throughout the Middle East, and US special operations forces cooperated with the Israeli military in planning and intelligence.

    [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

  - As Israel prepares for a possible ground attack on Gaza, the Biden administration and prominent members of Congress are preparing an aid package from the United States with about \$2 billion in additional funding to support Israel, Time reported.

    [^cite_note-351]

- On 12 October, Secretary of State Antony Blinken reiterated America's commitment to Israel's security during a news conference with Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu.

  [^cite_note-Harb-2023-23]



U.S. defense secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) meeting with Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) in the city of [Tel Aviv, Israel](31453.md), 13 October 2023

- On 14 October, Defense Secretary Lloyd J. Austin III announced that he had dispatched a second aircraft carrier to the eastern Mediterranean "to deter hostile actions against Israel or any efforts towards widening this war". In addition, the Air Force was sending twice as many F-16, A-10, and F-15E squadrons as before to the Persian Gulf region in order to intensify its land-based assault aircraft presence there. According to officials, the United States would have an aerial armada of more than 100 attack planes when combined with the four squadrons of F/A-18 jets that were stationed onboard each carrier. Additionally, to help with intelligence gathering and preparation for any operations aimed at locating and rescuing the 150 hostages—among them some Americans—that Hamas is keeping, the Pentagon had dispatched a small team of Special Operations personnel to Israel.

  [^cite_note-352]

- On 15 October, the White House declared that it would attempt this week to get congressional approval of a fresh \$2 billion weaponry aid package for Israel and Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-353]

  - Also US defense secretary Lloyd Austin ordered about 2,000 troops to be prepared for possible deployment to Israel, several defense officials said.

    [^cite_note-354]

    [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

  - According to three American officials and one Israeli official, the Biden administration was discussing about the possibility of using military force if Hezbollah joins the Gaza war.

    [^cite_note-355]

  - The United States was prepared to send more forces as a deterrent to the Middle East if necessary.

    [^cite_note-356]

- By 17 October, five shipments of American weapons and equipment had arrived in Israel.

  [^cite_note-Copp-2023-29]

  &#x20;The US put 2,000 troops on high alert and extended the deployment of an aircraft carrier in the Middle East.

  [^cite_note-357]



U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md), U.S. secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) and Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in [Tel Aviv, Israel](31453.md), 18 October 2023

- On 18 October, the US vetoed a [United Nations Security Council resolution](178970.md) that would have condemned the Hamas attack on Israel while calling for a pause in the fighting to allow humanitarian aid into Gaza. The US said the resolution did not do enough to underscore Israel's right to self-defense. The US ambassador to the UN, [Linda Thomas-Greenfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Thomas-Greenfield), told the council "We are on the ground doing the hard work of diplomacy. We believe we need to let that diplomacy play out".

  [^cite_note-358]

- On 19 October, one day after traveling to Israel, President Biden urged Congress to increase military aid to Israel, claiming that Hamas aimed to "annihilate" Israeli democracy.

  [^cite_note-359]

  [^cite_note-Holland-2023-16]

- On 20 October, Biden announced that the additional funds he asked Congress to authorize would come to a total of \$14 billion, as part of a \$105 billion military aid package that addressed Ukraine, Taiwan and Israel as well as US border security.

  [^cite_note-Beale-2023-360]

  [^cite_note-Holland-2023-16]

- On 21 October the Pentagon declared that two of its most potent missile defense systems—a [THAAD battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_High_Altitude_Area_Defense) and extra [Patriot batteries](52024.md)—would be deployed to the Middle East.

  [^cite_note-Beale-2023-360]

  [^cite_note-361]

- [Craig Mokhiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Mokhiber) of the [UN High Commission for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) resigned, criticising the organisation for its response to the Gaza war.

  [^cite_note-362]

  [^cite_note-FOOTNOTENareaSamuel2023-363]

  &#x20;He later said Israel's actions in Gaza are a "classic case of textbook genocide".

  [^cite_note-364]

#### November



The [USS *Dwight D. Eisenhower*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Dwight_D._Eisenhower) and the [USS *Gerald R. Ford*](4050542.md) carrier strike groups in November 2023

- A [Republican](32070.md) plan, approved by the United States House of Representatives, allocated almost \$14.5 billion in military aid for Israel.

  [^cite_note-365]

#### December



US secretary of defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin), Israeli defense minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), US Joint Chiefs of Staff Chairman [Charles Q. Brown Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Q._Brown_Jr.) and Israeli Chief of General Staff [Herzi Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzi_Halevi) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 18 December 2023

- A *[Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_Journal)* report says that the United States has given Israel a range of munitions, including so-called ["bunker buster" bombs](167751.md), for its war in Gaza. The report stated that US arms shipments to Israel since the start of the war included 15,000 bombs and 57,000 [155mm artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/155_mm_caliber), mostly carried on C-17 military cargo planes. U.S. has also sent more than 5,000 unguided [Mk82 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_82_bomb), more than 5,400 [Mk84 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_84_bomb), about 1,000 small diameter [GBU-39 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-39_Small_Diameter_Bomb), and almost 3,000 JDAMs. The *Wall Street Journal* says that some of the bloodiest Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip have involved the use of big US-made bombs, such as the one that destroyed an apartment complex in the Jabalia refugee camp and killed over a hundred people.

  [^cite_note-US_sends-2023-37]

- On 8 December, the US vetoed another UN Security Council resolution calling for an immediate humanitarian ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-Double_standards-2023-106]

  &#x20;The US called it "unbalanced" because it did not condemn the Hamas attack nor acknowledge Israel's right to self-defense.

  [^cite_note-366]

- On 9 December, the Pentagon said that on the prior day, Biden used emergency authority to skip congressional review to sell \~14,000 tank shells worth \$106.5 million for immediate delivery to Israel.

  [^cite_note-367]

- On 12 December, Biden said "Israel's security can rest on the United States" but warned that Israel was losing international support because of its "indiscriminate bombing" of Gaza.

  [^cite_note-368]

- On 29 December, the United States government again used emergency authority to sell Israel artillery shells and related weapons worth \$147.5 million in order to replenish Israeli weapons stockpiles.

  [^cite_note-369]

### 2024

#### January



US secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) and Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 9 January 2024

- On 4 January, [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)) stated the United States had "not seen anything" that Israel had done that would make the US change its approach.

  [^cite_note-370]

- On 18 January, US Department of State spokesman [Matthew Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Miller_\(spokesperson\)) stated, "Our support for Israel remains ironclad."

  [^cite_note-371]

- PM Netanyahu stated that he would not support a Palestinian state and that he was proud to have thus far prevented a state, leading Biden to comment that he believed a two-state solution was still possible with Netanyahu in power.

  [^cite_note-372]

- On 19 January, John Kirby stated, "We don't have any indications that there's deliberate efforts to commit war crimes" by Israel.

  [^cite_note-373]

- [Brett McGurk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brett_McGurk), the White House leader of post-war Gaza planning, was reportedly pushing a plan that would exchange minimal Israeli interference in the Palestinian Territories for Saudi normalization with Israel.

  [^cite_note-374]

- The [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA) established a new taskforce to provide intelligence to Israel regarding Hamas leaders.

  [^cite_note-375]

#### February



Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) with Israeli president Isaac Herzog at the [60th Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_Munich_Security_Conference), 16 February 2024

- On 8 February, Biden called Israel's actions in Gaza "over the top".

  [^cite_note-376]

  &#x20;Following this, the Biden administration issued a [national security directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_security_directive) demanding written assurances from Israel (and other countries) that it was using US-supplied weapons in line with international law or face a pause in weapons transfers.

  [^cite_note-377]

  [^cite_note-378]

  &#x20;Israel has to prove this to the US State Department on a yearly basis or risk having its weapons supplies cut.

  [^cite_note-379]

- In a call with [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) on 11 February, Biden said the US would not support an Israeli assault on Rafah without a "credible and executable plan" for ensuring the safety of civilians.

  [^cite_note-380]

  &#x20;Three anonymous U.S. officials stated that the US would not punish Israel if its forces attacked Rafah without a plan to protect civilians.

  [^cite_note-381]

- The State Department stated it was reviewing reports of Israel harming Palestinian civilians in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-382]

- The Senate passed a US\$14 billion aid package for Israel.

  [^cite_note-383]

- The U.S. began preparing to send Israel more weapons ahead of its planned [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive),

  [^cite_note-384]

  &#x20;stating, "Israel takes effective action to prevent gross violations of human rights."

  [^cite_note-385]

- On 20 February, the US [vetoed a UN Security Council resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_vetoed_United_Nations_Security_Council_resolutions) seeking an "immediate humanitarian ceasefire".

  [^cite_note-386]

  &#x20;The US envoy, Linda Thomas-Greenfield, said that the resolution would undermine ongoing hostage negotiations: "Demanding an immediate unconditional ceasefire, without an agreement requiring Hamas to release the hostages, will not bring about durable peace".

  [^cite_note-387]

  &#x20;Instead, the US proposed a draft UN Security Council resolution, calling for a "temporary ceasefire in Gaza as soon as practicable, based on the formula of all hostages being released". It stated that a [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) would have "serious implications" and "should not proceed under current circumstances". The US's draft resolution also rejects any Israeli attempt at demographic or territorial change in Gaza that would violate international law. The US said it would not yet put the resolution to a vote as it wanted to allow negotiations to continue.

  [^cite_note-388]

  [^cite_note-389]

- When asked about the deaths of Palestinian children, US Representative [Andy Ogles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Ogles) ([R](32070.md)) said: "I think we should kill 'em all ... Hamas and the Palestinians have been attacking Israel for 20 years. It's time to pay the piper."

  [^cite_note-390]

  &#x20;Supporters of Palestine, including the American Muslim Advisory Council, denounced his comments as a call for genocide.

  [^cite_note-391]

  [^cite_note-392]

- [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-immolation_of_Aaron_Bushnell) immolated himself in front of the [Embassy of Israel, Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_Washington,_D.C.) in protest of the war.

  [^cite_note-393]

- The [Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemkin_Institute_for_Genocide_Prevention) said the Biden administration was complicit in genocide in Gaza: "President Biden and key administration officials are on a path to be remembered as the principal enablers of one of the worst genocides in the 21st century."

  [^cite_note-394]

  &#x20;[Ali Harb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Harb) wrote, "US weapons have continued to flow to Israel to arm a military carrying out a suspected genocide in Gaza."

  [^cite_note-395]

* After the US vetoed a UN ceasefire resolution, Cuban president [Miguel Diaz-Canel Bermudez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Diaz-Canel_Bermudez) said, "They are accomplices of this genocide of Israel against Palestine."

  [^cite_note-396]

#### March

- Following the [Flour massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_massacre), the US began [airdrops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrops) of aid into Gaza.

  [^cite_note-397]

  &#x20;Officials stated, "Biden remains unwilling to make any major shifts in his policy toward Israel, including placing conditions on military aid to Israel".

  [^cite_note-398]

  - [Algeria](358.md) proposed a motion to the UN Security Council to release a statement condemning the massacre and blaming Israel, which was blocked by the United States. The deputy US ambassador to the UN, Robert Wood, said the US was alarmed by the incident and called on Israel to investigate, but claimed "We don't have all the facts on the ground".

    [^cite_note-399]

    

    Members of the US congressional delegation with Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in [Jerusalem](16043.md), 28 March 2024

- Biden called on Republicans to pass his foreign aid bill to "help ensure that Israel can defend itself". He said it would also provide "critical humanitarian aid to the Palestinian people".

  [^cite_note-400]

- On 4 March, US vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) called for "an immediate ceasefire for at least the next six weeks" because of "the immense scale of suffering in Gaza". She said Israel must let more aid into Gaza and called on Hamas to accept a ceasefire deal involving the release of hostages.

  [^cite_note-401]

  &#x20;She added that "President Joe Biden and I are unwavering in our commitment to Israel's security".

  [^cite_note-402]

  &#x20;Harris was reportedly urging Biden to speak more about the high death toll and plight of survivors in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-403]

- UN Ambassador Linda Thomas-Greenfield stated, "Not nearly enough humanitarian aid is getting into the hands of Palestinians".

  [^cite_note-404]

- U.S. officials told Congress they had approved more than 100 arms sales to Israel since 7 October.

  [^cite_note-405]

- Some of Biden's closest allies in the U.S. Senate — including [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons), [Jack Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Reed_\(Rhode_Island_politician\)), [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine), and [Patty Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patty_Murray) — were reportedly pressuring Biden to change his tactics in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-406]

  &#x20;Senator Bernie Sanders, along with seven other U.S. senators, warned Biden that arming Israel was a violation of the [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act), which bars the U.S. from arming countries that limit humanitarian aid.

  [^cite_note-407]

  [^cite_note-408]

- An intelligence report found that Israel was likely to experience armed resistance from Hamas "for years to come" and that Netanyahu's viability as leader "may be in jeopardy".

  [^cite_note-409]

- In a speech on 14 March, the Democratic US [Senate majority leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_majority_leader), [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer), berated Israeli PM Netanyahu as an "obstacle to peace". He said that Netanyahu "has been too willing to tolerate the civilian toll in Gaza" and that "Israel cannot survive if it becomes a pariah".

  [^cite_note-CBS_News-2024-410]

  &#x20;Schumer added that if Netanyahu stayed in power after the war, "the United States will have no choice but to play a more active role in shaping Israeli policy by using our leverage to change the present course".

  [^cite_note-411]

  &#x20;President Biden praised the speech and said Schumer's "serious concerns" are shared by many Americans.

  [^cite_note-CBS_News-2024-410]

- A group of 19 Senate Democrats sent an open letter to Biden, stating, "We request the Biden administration promptly establish a bold, public framework outlining the steps necessary" to create a Palestinian state.

  [^cite_note-412]

- [Jack Lew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Lew), Joseph R. Biden's ambassador to Israel, endorsed Israel's claim that it was abiding by international law during its war on Gaza.

  [^cite_note-413]

- [Reuters](18998750.md) reported congressional leaders and the White House had reached a deal to bar funding for [UNRWA](32270.md), the organization principally responsible for delivering food and providing education in Gaza, until March 2025.

  [^cite_note-414]

  - Upon the bill's passage, Democratic U.S. Senator Chris Van Hollen stated, "Denying funding for UNRWA is tantamount to denying food to starving people and restricting medical supplies to injured civilians".

    [^cite_note-415]

  - The bill included a long-standing provision that limits aid to the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) if "the Palestinians initiate an International Criminal Court (ICC) judicially authorized investigation, or actively supports such an investigation, that subjects Israeli nationals to an investigation for alleged crimes against Palestinians."

    [^cite_note-416]

- [John Barrasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Barrasso) stated that in a meeting with Netanyahu, senate Republicans told him that "Israel has every right to defend themselves".

  [^cite_note-417]

- The US put forward a draft UN Security Council resolution which stated the "imperative" for "an immediate and sustained ceasefire", facilitating aid delivery and supporting ongoing talks between Israel and Hamas, linked to the release of hostages.

  [^cite_note-418]

  &#x20;On 22 March, the draft resolution was vetoed by Russia and China, who said the wording was ambiguous and was not a straightforward "call" or "demand" to halt hostilities.

  [^cite_note-419]

  

  US secretary of state Antony Blinken with Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and the [Israeli war cabinet](75030844.md) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 22 March 2024



US secretary of state Antony Blinken and [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz) in Tel Aviv, 22 March 2024

- On 22 March, U.S. Secretary of State Antony Blinken said he told the Israeli government that "A major military ground operation \[in Rafah] is not the way to do it. It risks killing many more civilians, it risks wreaking greater havoc on the provision of humanitarian assistance, it risks further isolation of Israel around the world and jeopardizing its long-term security and standing". He added "We're looking forward to seeing Israeli officials in Washington next week to go through the details and the best way forward. We're determined that Israel succeed in defending itself".

  [^cite_note-420]

- On 23 March, the US Defense Ministry said a planned visit by the Israeli Defense Minister to the U.S. would focus on "the progress of the fighting to dismantle Hamas in the Gaza Strip, the efforts to return the hostages held captive by Hamas, the actions being taken on the ground to bring in humanitarian aid", as well as on weapons procurement "to preserve the qualitative advantage of the State of Israel in the region".

  [^cite_note-421]

- On 24 March, Vice president Kamala Harris replied, "I am ruling out nothing," when asked if there would be consequences for Israel if it invaded Rafah.

  [^cite_note-422]

- A group of 17 Democratic senators urged the Biden administration to reject Israel's report stating that it was complying with international human rights law in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-423]

  &#x20;The following day, the U.S. Department of State accepted Israel's statements regarding its compliance.

  [^cite_note-424]

- On 25 March, the UN Security Council passed a resolution demanding an immediate ceasefire for the remainder of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan), "leading to a lasting sustainable ceasefire", and demanding the immediate and unconditional release of all hostages. The US abstained, allowing the resolution to pass.

  [^cite_note-425]

  &#x20;The Israeli government berated the US for not voting against the resolution, and called off a meeting between an Israeli delegation and US officials in Washington.

  [^cite_note-426]

- The U.S. House introduced a bill to reevaluate the U.S. relationship with South Africa.

  [^cite_note-427]

- On 30 March, the White House authorized \$2.5 billion in weapons transfers to Israel.

  [^cite_note-428]

- Republican congressman [Tim Walberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Walberg) suggested dropping a nuclear bomb on Gaza to "get it over quick".

  [^cite_note-429]

- [Donald Trump](4848272.md) said that Biden "dumped Israel" due to being overly influenced by [pro-Palestinian protests](75347645.md), that he supported Israel's ongoing offensive in Gaza, that Israel had to "finish the problem", and that the [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration) "got soft", which some commentators viewed as a call to continue and "double down" on genocidal acts. Trump's campaign also said that, if elected, he would bar Gaza residents from entering the US.

  [^cite_note-430]

  [^cite_note-431]

  [^cite_note-432]

  [^cite_note-433]

  [^cite_note-434]

  [^cite_note-435]

#### April

- In response to a question asking whether additional weapons transfers to Israel would weaken U.S. credibility, a State Department spokesman stated, "We are committed to Israel's right to self-defence, and this is a long-term commitment the United States has made."

  [^cite_note-436]

- Following the [World Central Kitchen drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_drone_strikes), a senior U.S. official told *Politico*, "It's just rinse and repeat with the Israelis. The American political system can't or won't draw a real line with them and that is regrettable."

  [^cite_note-437]

- U.S. senator [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons) stated, "If Benjamin Netanyahu were to order the IDF into Rafah at scale... and make no provision for civilians or for humanitarian aid, I would vote to condition aid to Israel".

  [^cite_note-438]

- Former Speaker of the House [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) signed a letter urging Biden to stop sending arms to Israel.

  [^cite_note-439]

- On 9 April, U.S. defense secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) told the Senate Armed Services Committee that the Pentagon had no evidence that Israel was carrying out a [genocide against Palestinians in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza).

  [^cite_note-440]

  &#x20;He said there was "no question" that there had been "far too many" [civilian casualties](75130785.md) in the war in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-441]

- A report by [ProPublica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProPublica) found that U.S. secretary of state [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) had failed to take action on State Department recommendations that units in the Israeli army be sanctioned due to their involvement with rapes and killings.

  [^cite_note-442]

- A U.S. State Department report described "allegations of numerous incidents such as arbitrary or unlawful killings, enforced disappearance, torture and unjustified arrests of journalists".

  [^cite_note-443]

- Biden signed a [\$95 billion security package](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Act,_2024), which included around \$26 billion in military aid for Israel and \$1 billion in humanitarian aid for Gaza.

  [^cite_note-444]

  [^cite_note-445]

- On 24 April, twelve [Republicans](32070.md) in the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md); [Tom Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Cotton), [Marco Rubio](5502549.md), [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz), [Mitch McConnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_McConnell), [Marsha Blackburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsha_Blackburn), [Katie Boyd Britt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Boyd_Britt), [Ted Budd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Budd), [Kevin Cramer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Cramer), [Bill Hagerty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Hagerty), [Pete Ricketts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Ricketts), [Rick Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Scott), and [Tim Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Scott), sent a letter to the [International Criminal Court](14880.md)'s chief prosecutor, [Karim Ahmad Khan](11606957.md), that warned him that any attempt by the ICC to [pursue charges against Israeli officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine) over [war crimes committed in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza) will be interpreted "not only as a threat to Israel's sovereignty but to the sovereignty of the United States". The senators told Khan, "Target Israel and we will target you ... \[and] sanction your employees and associates, and bar you and your families from the United States. ... You have been warned."

- Responding to the Republican senators, [Democratic](5043544.md) Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) of Maryland said, "It is fine to express opposition to a possible judicial action, but it is absolutely wrong to interfere in a judicial matter by threatening judicial officers, their family members and their employees with retribution. This thuggery is something befitting the [mafia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mafia), not U.S. senators."

  [^cite_note-EXCLUSIVE-446]

  [^cite_note-447]

  [^cite_note-448]

- A joint assessment from the [Bureau of Democracy, Human Rights, and Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Democracy,_Human_Rights,_and_Labor), the [Bureau of Population, Refugees, and Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Population,_Refugees,_and_Migration); the [Office of Global Criminal Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Global_Criminal_Justice) and the [Bureau of International Organization Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_International_Organization_Affairs) found that Israel is potentially violating international humanitarian law in Gaza and that Israel's claims that they are using U.S.-supplied weaponry in accordance with such laws are neither "credible or reliable".

  [^cite_note-449]

- The U.S. Department of State found five Israeli army units responsible for "gross violations of human rights" prior to 7 October and that they remained eligible for military aid.

  [^cite_note-450]

#### May



Blinken and Gallant at the [Kerem Shalom border crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) in Kerem Shalom, Israel, 1 May 2024

- On 1 May 2024, U.S. senators met International Criminal Court officials online to discuss potential arrest warrants.

  [^cite_note-451]

  &#x20;On 3 May 2024, chief prosecutor Karim Ahmad Khan called for "all attempts to impede, intimidate, or improperly influence officials \[to] cease immediately" and referred to [Article 70](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Rome%20Statute%20of%20the%20International%20Criminal%20Court#Article_70:_Offences_against_the_administration_of_justice) of the [Rome Statute](68762.md) defining "retaliating against an official of the court on account of duties performed" as an offence.

  [^cite_note-452]

  [^cite_note-453]

  [^cite_note-EXCLUSIVE-446]

- Tony Blinken stated the "only thing standing between the people of Gaza and a ceasefire is Hamas".

  [^cite_note-454]

  &#x20;Two days later, Hamas agreed to a U.S.-negotiated ceasefire,

  [^cite_note-455]

  &#x20;which Israel then rejected.

  [^cite_note-456]

- A letter signed by 86 House representatives stated that Israel's restrictions on humanitarian aid called into question its assurances of compliance with the U.S. Foreign Assistance Act.

  [^cite_note-457]

- The U.S. president stated it would halt the shipment of bombs to Israel if a "major invasion" of Rafah were launched, stating, "Civilians have been killed in Gaza as a consequence of those bombs".

  [^cite_note-458]

  &#x20;Less than a week later, however, Biden notified Congress about a \$1 billion arms sale to Israel.

  [^cite_note-459]

- A U.S. State Department report found Israel's use of U.S. weapons "likely" violated international law.

  [^cite_note-460]

  &#x20;In the same report, however, the U.S. found Israel's assurances it was following international law to be "credible".

  [^cite_note-461]

  &#x20;[Avril Benoit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avril_Benoit), the executive director of [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md), stated the analysis was not a "good faith effort to uphold US law".

  [^cite_note-462]

- On 12 May 2024, Republican presidential candidate Donald Trump stated, "(Biden) is surrendering our college campuses to anarchists, jihadist freaks and anti-american extremists who are trying to tear down our American flag. ... If you come here from another country and try to bring jihadism or anti-Americanism or anti-Semitism to our campuses, we will immediately deport you. You'll be out of that school."

  [^cite_note-463]

- The Republican-controlled U.S. House passed legislation that would slash the U.S. military budget unless Biden sent 3,500 heavy-duty bombs to Israel.

  [^cite_note-464]

  &#x20;The White House said Biden would veto it if it come to his desk.

  [^cite_note-465]

- US president Joe Biden said that the request of the prosecutor of the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) to issue an arrest warrant against the leaders of Israel is cruel and the U.S. will always stand by Israel.

  [^cite_note-466]

- 31 May – Biden announces [an Israeli ceasefire proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war_ceasefire_proposal).

  [^cite_note-467]

#### June

- US lawmakers have passed legislation aimed at sanctioning the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) over efforts by its prosecutors to issue arrest warrants for senior Israeli officials accused of [war crimes in Gaza](75113830.md).

  [^cite_note-468]

- The [Nuseirat rescue operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Nuseirat_rescue_operation) was aided by intelligence support from the United States.

  [^cite_note-469]

  [^cite_note-470]

- [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) was invited to address a joint session of Congress, scheduled for 24 July 2024.

  [^cite_note-471]

- The United States officially signed a Letter of Offer and Acceptance, allowing Israel to [purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_Israeli_procurement) 25 additional [Lockheed Martin F-35](11812.md) stealth fighter jets for \$3 billion.

  [^cite_note-472]

- Rep. [Gregory Meeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_Meeks) and Sen. [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin) signed off to an arms sale to Israel totaling \$18 billion, after pressure from the Biden administration.

  [^cite_note-473]

- The U.S. House passed a bill barring the U.S. State Department from using the Gaza Health Ministry's death toll statistics.

  [^cite_note-474]

#### July

- U.S. vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) stated that she would not preside over Netanyahu's joint session of Congress.

  [^cite_note-475]

- The United States has resumed shipments of the 500-pound bombs, which were halted in February over concerns about the humanitarian impact of Israel's use of them in killing Palestinians in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-476]

- Netanyahu made an address to a joint session of Congress, amidst large protests in Washington.

  [^cite_note-477]

  [^cite_note-478]

- Following a meeting with Netanyahu, U.S. vice president Harris stated she would "not be silent" about the suffering of Palestinians in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-479]

- The Department of State criticized the [ICJ advisory opinion on the Israeli occupation](72240449.md), claiming that it would make future negotiations for a Palestinian state more difficult.

  [^cite_note-480]

#### August



US [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Chiefs_of_Staff) Chairman [Charles Q. Brown Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Q._Brown_Jr.) and Israeli Chief of General Staff [Herzi Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzi_Halevi) in [Bnei Brak](314671.md), Israel, 26 August 2024

- Kamala Harris' national security advisor [Philip H. Gordon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_H._Gordon) said that Harris did not support an [arms embargo on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_embargo_on_Israel).

  [^cite_note-481]

- U.S. ambassador [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) announced he would skip the atomic bombing peace memorial in Nagasaki because of the city's decision not to invite Israel to the ceremony, along with Russia and Belarus.

  [^cite_note-482]

- On 9 August 2024, the Department of State said the United States would send Israel an additional \$3.5 billion to spend on US-made weapons and military equipment.

  [^cite_note-483]

- On 13 August 2024, the Department of State announced that the U.S. had approved a \$20 billion weapons package sale to Israel, which included fighter jets and advanced air-to-air missiles.

  [^cite_note-484]

- The U.S. government was sued for allegedly discriminating against Palestinian American activists, including seizing personal electronic devices and placing one plaintiff on the [No Fly List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_Fly_List).

  [^cite_note-485]

- Former president and Republican presidential nominee Donald Trump claimed that he told Benjamin Netanyahu, "It has to get over with fast... Get your victory and get it over with. It has to stop, the killing has to stop." He also made the statement, "From the start, Harris has worked to tie Israel's hand behind its back, demanding an immediate ceasefire, always demanding ceasefire," and added that it "would only give Hamas time to regroup and launch a new October 7 style attack". Trump also labelled pro-Palestinian supporters calling for an end to U.S. support for Israel's war crimes as "pro-Hamas thugs" and "jihad sympathizers". He threatened to arrest and deport them from the United States if he becomes president again.

  [^cite_note-486]

- Following the Biden administration's decision not to sanction the [Netzah Yehuda Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netzah_Yehuda_Battalion),

  [^cite_note-487]

  &#x20;former [State Department](31975.md) human rights official Charles Blaha stated, "It really calls into question how much value the department gives to the lives of Palestinian-Americans."

  [^cite_note-488]

- During the [2024 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Democratic_National_Convention), anti-war protesters called for a ceasefire and arms embargo against Israel.

  [^cite_note-489]

#### September–October

- The city council of [Portland, Maine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portland,_Maine) passed a resolution urging the city to divest from 80 companies which were potentially complicit in Israel's violations of international law.

  [^cite_note-490]

- U.S. representative [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib) submitted a list of the children killed in Gaza into the official record of the United States Congress.

  [^cite_note-491]

- The U.S. secretary of state and Chairman of the Joints Chiefs of Staff wrote a letter to Israel threatening that weapons transfers would be affected if it didn't increase humanitarian aid into northern Gaza within 30 days.

  [^cite_note-492]

#### November



Herzog and Biden at the White House in Washington, D.C., 12 November 2024

- On 12 November, Israeli President Herzog arrived in Washington, D.C. for a state visit and met with President Joe Biden. He told Biden: "In the Bible, it says that [Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_\(Genesis\)) will strengthen Israel. And clearly, Mr. President, you've done that."

  [^cite_note-493]

- The Biden administration said on 12 November that Israel has successfully increased the flow of humanitarian aid to Gaza and will continue to receive arms transfers from US. The United States had threatened a month ago if the situation had not improved, it would halt arm transfer to Israel. The relief groups are of the opinion that conditions are now worse than they were at any point during the 13-month-old war.

  [^cite_note-494]

- HuffPost reported that the Biden administration persuaded Democratic senators to vote against a resolution to halt the sale of tank shells to Israel, requested by prominent progressives and mainstream Democratic senators. The resolution failed by a vote of 79 to 18.

  [^cite_note-495]

### 2025

#### February



US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) receiving Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) at the White House on 7 April 2025

- On 4 February 2025, in a joint press conference with Benjamin Netanyahu, President Trump proposed that the [United States "take over" the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal) and re-develop it.

- On 28 February 2025, President Trump approved an over \$2.5 billion arms sale to Israel, which included 35,500 [MK 84 and BLU-117 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_84_bomb) and 4,000 Predator warheads.

  [^cite_note-496]

#### March

- Instead of continuing to the [second phase of the ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire#Second_stage) per the original agreement, Israel proposed a new plan (called the "Witkoff plan" after Steve Witkoff), in which Hamas would release the Israeli captives in exchange for a 50-day extension of the ceasefire, with Israel retaining the option of returning to war. Hamas rejected this new proposal, which differed from the terms agreed in January 2025.

  [^cite_note-497]

  [^cite_note-498]

- On 23 March 2025, President Trump's Middle East Special Envoy [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) blamed Hamas for [renewed fighting in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip),

  [^cite_note-499]

  &#x20;saying that "Hamas had every opportunity to demilitarize, to accept the bridging proposal that would have given us a 40- or 50-day ceasefire where we could have discussed demilitarization and a final truce."

  [^cite_note-500]



[CIA](5183633.md) Director [John Ratcliffe](42699041.md) and Trump's Middle East Envoy [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) with Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu, 7 July 2025

#### June

- On 1 June 2025, the IDF killed at least 32 civilians and wounded over 200 at the aid centre in Rafah.

  [^cite_note-501]

  &#x20;Pro-Palestinian social media accounts referred to the 1 June incident as the "*Witkoff massacre*", referring to US Middle East envoy [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md), who had endorsed Israel's plan to take over aid delivery in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-502]

* In June 2025, the UN Security Council adopted a resolution urging the immediate and unconditional lifting of all restrictions on the [entry of food and humanitarian aid](76066918.md) into [Gaza](55387.md), citing the city's “catastrophic” situation. Fourteen countries voted in favor of the resolution, while the United States exercised its veto.

  [^cite_note-503]

- In June 2025, American [evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) leader and businessman [Johnnie Moore Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnnie_Moore_Jr.) was appointed the executive chairman of the [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) (GHS).

  [^cite_note-504]

  &#x20;The functioning of the organization has been marked by [repeated mass killings](80075155.md) in the vicinity of GHF distribution sites.

  [^cite_note-505]

#### July

#### August

- US President Donald Trump said on Tuesday that the full occupation of Gaza was "pretty much up to Israel".

  [^cite_note-506]

- On 16 August 2025, the US State Department has announced that it has suspended the issuance of visitor visas to people from the Gaza Strip, including children who require urgent medical care.

  [^cite_note-507]

#### September



Trump at the [Gaza peace summit](81324529.md) in [Sharm El Sheikh](419335.md), Egypt, 13 October 2025

- The US State Department has sanctioned three Palestinian human rights groups that have called on the International Criminal Court to investigate allegations of war crimes in Gaza and arrest Israeli leaders.

  [^cite_note-508]

- On 29 September 2025, Trump announced, alongside Benjamin Netanyahu, a [20-Point Gaza Peace Plan](81215079.md) from the White House which consisted of 20 specific points aimed at achieving a ceasefire, the return of [Israeli hostages](75025451.md), dismantling Hamas's military capabilities, and establishing a transitional governance structure in the Gaza Strip.

  [^cite_note-509]

#### October

- Trump announced his deal for a ceasefire between Israel and Hamas had been reached and that the remaining hostages would be released.

  [^cite_note-510]

- British journalist Sami Hamdi was detained by federal immigration authorities at San Francisco International Airport on October 26 for criticizing Israel's ongoing military operation in Gaza while giving a speech in the United States, according to [Council on American–Islamic Relations](159138.md).

  [^cite_note-511]

## Pro-Israel lobby

See also: [Israel lobby in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_lobby_in_the_United_States)



Secretary of Defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) speaks at the AIPAC Political Leadership Forum in Washington, D.C., 2023

On 30 October 2023, [AIPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIPAC), a leading pro-Israel lobbying group, issued a public critique of the members of Congress who voted against House Resolution 771, which expressed support for Israel. In response, Republican [Thomas Massie](28005891.md) defended his vote by saying he objected to a broad "open-ended promise of military support," while Democrats [Cori Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cori_Bush), [Mark Pocan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Pocan) and [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez) accused AIPAC of harming US democracy.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] On 1 November, [Ilhan Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilhan_Omar) accused AIPAC of running Islamophobic ads against her.[^cite_note-515] On 2 November, [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib), who is of Palestinian descent, was the target of a \$100,000 TV ad campaign by the [Democratic Majority for Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Majority_for_Israel).[^cite_note-516]

The Republican-controlled House of Representatives on 5 December passed a resolution that included language that said the House "clearly and firmly states that anti-Zionism is antisemitism". The resolution also condemned the slogan "[From the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea),"[^cite_note-517] for which Tlaib was censured on 5 November.[^cite_note-518] Analysis from *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* showed that Congress members who were supportive of Israel from the beginning of the war had received an average of \$100,000 more from pro-Israel donors than their pro-Palestinian colleagues.[^cite_note-519] Analysis of [Federal Election Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Election_Commission) filings showed House Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) received \$95,000 from AIPAC.[^cite_note-520]

In March 2024, a group of 20 progressive political organizations formed an anti-AIPAC coalition to push back against the lobbyist group's influence on U.S. politics.[^cite_note-521] In response to comments by Senator Chuck Schumer that Israel needed new elections to replace Netanyahu, [J Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J_Street) stated the speech "signals a historic shift from those in the Democratic party who care deeply about Israel's future".[^cite_note-522] *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* found that hundreds of fake social media accounts were targeting [Democratic Party](5043544.md) lawmakers with messages repeating Israeli government accusations relating to [UNRWA](32270.md) and [Hamas](13913.md).[^cite_note-523] *Haaretz* found Israel targeted U.S. lawmakers with an influence campaign, primarily focused on Black politicians.[^cite_note-524] In July 2024, a group of more than 30 human rights organizations requested the Biden administration investigate these influence campaigns.[^cite_note-525] *[Politico](9172097.md)* found that AIPAC was the largest source of Republican donors' money spent in 2024 Democratic primary races.[^cite_note-526]

In an early June 2024 interview by the former [Fox News](11121.md) host [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson), the Republican U.S. Representative [Thomas Massie](28005891.md) stated that AIPAC has designated representatives assigned to each Republican member of Congress to ensure that the lawmakers vote for bills that Israel supports. He also questioned why the lobby group and their representatives are not required to register as [foreign agents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Agents_Registration_Act), which would be the case for those advocating on behalf of all other countries.[^cite_note-527][^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529]

## Antisemitism Awareness Act

Main article: [Antisemitism Awareness Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_Awareness_Act)

The [Antisemitism Awareness Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_Awareness_Act), spearheaded by the Republicans but also backed by many Democrats, passed the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) in a 320–91 vote on 1 May 2024, and proceeded to the Senate.[^cite_note-530][^cite_note-531]
